## CORRECTIONS-11

Dan, 21 November, 1:38 AM ET: "when it moves from one category to the other can we do a transition animation on the left table?" `CORRECTIONS-11.md` (C1) controls this round: when the craft changes, a red hairline wipes across the new row, carrying its colour change, and the old row clears the same way. Rounds 2–10 are kept. Only files in `take-2/` changed: the new `craft-wipe.mjs` and `tools/round11-checks.mjs`; `play.mjs`, `take-2.css`, `tools/checks.mjs`, `tools/entrance-checks.mjs` and `tools/round8-checks.mjs` (their player stand-ins now supply `createCraftWipe`), this file and README. `public/` is read only. LOOP is still 91.498 s; the loop, the scene, the stars, the name and the entrance's timing are untouched.

### C1: the craft list's wipe (`craft-wipe.mjs`, `take-2.css`, `play.mjs`)

- **One span per row.** Each row's state is one span `[lo, hi]`, in fractions of the row's width, that holds both its red rule and its "on" colours: `[0, 1]` is the current row at rest, `[0, 0]` a row at rest off. Drawing is `[0, front]`, clearing `[front, 1]`. Because the rule and the text share the span, the colour change is the same hard edge as the rule's end.
- **Timetable** (`CRAFT_WIPE`): the old row clears over 0.35 s from the change with `EASE.wipe` (imported from `public/journey/hero/engine.mjs`, as `entrance.mjs` does); the new row draws from +0.1 s to +0.5 s with `EASE.wipe`. Within 1e-9 of its end a wipe is over, so the list is exactly at rest at +0.5 s.
- **Drawn with custom properties only.** `li::before` is the rule: absolutely placed 1 px tall at `top: -1px`, exactly over the `border-top` hairline (at `top: 0`, the cell's top edge, in the phone grid, which has no hairlines), cut by `clip-path: inset(0 calc(100% - var(--wb) * 100%) 0 calc(var(--wa) * 100%))`. The word is painted by a hard-stop `linear-gradient` (`--off`, then paper from `--xa` to `--xb`, then `--off`) under `background-clip: text` with a transparent text fill, so the front is a hard edge through the letters. `--xa/--xb` are the span in the word's own px (`lo × row width − word left`). The number is muted or red by `.is-lit` on the row, which is set when the front has passed the number's box right edge (the same edge the entrance cuts it in at). The `li` gains `position: relative` (no offsets). No padding, border, font or text node changes: the overlay is generated content and the word keeps its own text, so `page-layout.mjs`'s ink measures are unchanged.
- **Hover and focus.** `--off` is muted, or paper under `:hover` (`@media (hover: hover)`), so a hovered row still lights as today; the focus ring and `aria-current` (still set by `play.mjs` each frame) are untouched. The `.n` colour rules are written above journey.css's `.hero-crafts button[aria-current] .n` (0,3,1) so the number follows the wipe, not `aria-current`.
- **What triggers it.** `play.mjs`'s `draw()` hands the scene's craft to `craftWipe.follow(craft, { cut: !!intro })`. `followCraft()` sets the list at once on the first frame, during the entrance and with reduced motion, and otherwise any new craft is a change: the loop moving on (44.285, 60.967, 76.049 s and 3 to 0 at the wrap), a craft click (`choose()`), a stage click, and also `__hero.seek` and a click during the entrance (which ends it, then changes). It always runs from the model's own current craft (`const from = current;`), so 01 to 03 clears 01, draws 03 and leaves 02 alone.
- **A change during a change.** A row still clearing finishes clearing on its schedule. The row that was drawing clears from where it is: its span becomes `[max(lo, front), hi]` with the clear's front sweeping on the full-width 0.35 s timetable, so it is empty as soon as the front reaches where the draw stopped. The newest row draws from the start (if it was itself still clearing, it cuts empty and draws from +0.1 s). At most one row is ever `[0, 1]`.
- **Its clock.** `performance.now()`, with its own `requestAnimationFrame` loop that runs only while a wipe is busy (like `name-react.mjs`), so it finishes while the loop is paused and with the stage held. `__hero.crafts(p)` holds the latest change at progress p (0–1, time = change + p × 0.5 s; a change made while held shows at the same p); `__hero.crafts(null)` lets it run on from there. `__hero.craftWipe` reads `{ current, changes, held, busy, spans, lit }`.
- **First load.** During the entrance the list is set, not changed, so the current row's rule sits at rest under the entrance's own clip on the `li` (`rect(0, -1, tip, 1)`), which draws it with its hairline in the same wipe at the same time. The loop's start after the entrance is the same craft, so no change. Reduced motion: every change cuts to the rest state. Without scripts the list has no `.is-wiped` class and no current row, as today.

### Departures and readings

- **"The number cuts as the front passes it"** is read as the front passing the right edge of the number's 1.6 em box, as the entrance does (`numberRight`). In the 1440 × 900 row (380 px) that is at front 0.088: the old number cuts at +0.070 s and the new one at +0.175 s.
- **"Finishes clearing or cuts clear"**: finishes clearing was chosen.
- **The text's colour change uses a hard-stop `background-clip: text` fill** rather than a second clipped copy of the text, so no new text nodes reach the list's ink measurement. The brief's "clipped overlay" is used for the rule.
- **With a real mouse click** the pointer is over the new row, so its off colour is the hover paper and only the rule and the number show the draw on that row (filmed in `paused-01-03.png`). The other films click by `element.click()` with the pointer away, to show the text's front.

## CORRECTIONS-12: S1 and L2

Dan, 21 November, 2:35–2:37 AM ET: "can we make it so the treshold of stars collected is higher before it turns into the logo?" and "can we do something more creative than the line for the table?" This section covers only S1 and L2 of `CORRECTIONS-12.md`; D1, I1 and X1 were built separately and are recorded on their own. Rounds 2–11 are kept except where S1 and L2 change them (L2 replaces round 11's C1). Files changed: `stars.mjs`, `craft-wipe.mjs` (rewritten; file name and exports kept so the player and the stand-ins in `checks.mjs`, `entrance-checks.mjs` and `round8-checks.mjs` keep their seams), `take-2.css`, `play.mjs`, `tools/round9-checks.mjs`, `tools/checks.mjs`, the new `tools/round12-stars-list-checks.mjs`, this file and README; `tools/round11-checks.mjs` is removed (its wipe model is gone; its kept rules moved into the round 12 checks). `stage.mjs`, `art.mjs`, `labels.mjs`, `view.mjs` and `public/` are untouched. LOOP is still 91.498 s.

### S1: the logo forms once 300 stars are gathered (`stars.mjs`)

- **The rule.** `STARS.mark.threshold = 300` replaces `STARS.mark.at = 1.4`. In `gather()`, the formation starts (`hold.formedAt`) in the frame the cluster reaches 300 captured; from then on it is round 9's formation unchanged (later captures take the next free points, overflow turns in the ring, round 10's colours and fades). With a still pointer at the middle of a 1440 × 900 hero (720, 450) it starts at 2.400 s held (301 caught, radius 329.6 px); at (700, 450) 2.383 s. A uniform field holds 300 stars inside 321 px, which the radius reaches at 2.32 s; this field's hash has a few fewer stars there, hence 2.38–2.40 s, "about 2.3 s".
- **Holds near an edge.** `holdRadius(held, extra, diagonal) = min(diagonal, captureRadius(held) + extra)`; while a mark exists and the formation has not started, `extra = max(0, held − 2.5) × 104` (`growthRate()` = 260 / 2.5). Once formed, `extra` is frozen, so the radius stops. In the four corners the formation starts at 5.33–5.38 s held with radii of 635–640 px (a quarter circle). A hero with fewer than 300 stars (600 × 400 has 222) grows to its diagonal (721.1 px, at 6.167 s) and forms with what it has. `captureRadius()` itself is unchanged (round 8's 80–340 px), and so is the blast: its impulse and its weak-push reach still use `captureRadius(held)` with the 2.5 s cap. Without a mark (`createField(W, H)` with no paths) there is no formation and no extra growth, as round 8.
- **Reading.** "Once the formation starts, the radius stops growing" is read as stopping the extra growth past 2.5 s. A middle hold forms before 2.5 s and its radius goes on along round 8's curve to 340 px at 2.5 s, as before; stopping it at 2.4 s instead would have changed the middle's gathering, which the brief keeps.
- **Exposed.** `__hero.stars.radius` (the hold's current radius, null when not holding) and `__hero.stars.mark.captured`.
- **Round 9's checks follow the new start.** `tools/round9-checks.mjs` now checks "no point before the formation starts, identical to round 8 until then, first points in the starting frame", and "every star given a point at the start is within 1 px 1 s later" (1.25 s for the corner holds, whose stars start up to about 640 px out instead of 340: the formation's τ is unchanged). The colour and release holds run to 3.5 s instead of 2.5 s (the start moved from 1.4 to about 2.4 s), and the stage presses are 0.1, 0.24, 0.3, 1.3, 2.0 (no mark) and 3.0 s (mark, 320 stars). Its "formation off" control now edits `if (mark && hold.formedAt !== null) {`.

### L2: the selected row (`craft-wipe.mjs`, `take-2.css`, `play.mjs`)

- **At rest.** The list's `::before` is a 1 px `var(--red-bright)` (`#F0484F`) border box from `--sl/--st` to `--sr/--sb`, each a line's own pixel position in px from the list's top left. Desktop: the top line is the row's own top hairline, the bottom line the next row's top hairline (the last row's own bottom hairline), the sides at the row's left edge and right edge − 1. Phones (no hairlines): just inside the current cell. `::after` is the top left handle, 5 × 5 px solid red, at (`--sl` − 2, `--st` − 2), so it is centred on the corner pixel; its three `box-shadow`s at (`--sr` − `--sl`, 0), (0, `--sb` − `--st`) and both are the other three. Square corners, no fades. The list gets `position: relative`; both pseudo-elements are absolute, so nothing moves. Measured at 1440 × 900 the rows are 380 × 34.594 px (the last 35.594) and the rectangle for 01 is l 0, r 379, t 0, b 34.594; on the phone grid the cells are 171 × 44.391 px.
- **Colours.** `.is-lit` on the row: text paper, number red; otherwise the button inherits muted (paper under hover, as before). These rules sit above journey.css's `aria-current` colours (0,2,1 and 0,3,1), so the colours follow the selection's cuts while `aria-current` still moves at once. Round 11's `li::before` rule, `--off`, `--wa/--wb/--xa/--xb` and the `background-clip: text` fill are removed.
- **A change.** The model keeps four edges (l, r, t, b). On a change at time T, each edge goes from where it is at T to the new row's value: an edge moving outward (the max edges growing, the min edges shrinking: the side the selection moves toward) leads over T to T + 0.3 s; an edge moving inward trails over T + 0.1 to T + 0.45 s; both `EASE.wipe`, exactly on target within 1e-9 of the end (`CRAFT_SELECT`). Desktop 01 to 02: the bottom edge leads, the top trails. Phones move both axes at once by the same rule (01 to 04: right and bottom lead, left and top trail; 02 to 03: left and bottom lead). A change during a change is the same rule applied from where each edge is; an edge that does not move stays. An outward edge never crosses an inward one, so the rectangle never turns inside out (checked).
- **The cuts.** The new row lights when the selection covers it, which is when its leading edge reaches the row's far side (+0.3 s). Any row that was lit stays lit while the selection overlaps it and cuts back when it no longer does, which is when the trailing edge leaves it (01 to 02: +0.45 s, the trailing top edge reaching 01's bottom hairline; 04 to 01: +0.205 s, the bottom edge passing 04's top hairline; phone cells about +0.315 s). Rows passed over are never lit, and a row that was the target of an interrupted change but never covered stays off. Cover and overlap are exact (1e-6 px), since edges land exactly on their targets and desktop rows share a hairline.
- **Kept from round 11.** Own clock (`performance.now()` and its own rAF while busy), `__hero.crafts(p)` (time = change + p × 0.45 s) and `__hero.crafts(null)`, every trigger through `followCraft()`, always from the selection's actual place, the entrance and the loop's start make no change, reduced motion cuts, `aria-current`, hover and the focus ring. `__hero.craftWipe` now reads `{ current, changes, held, busy, rect, rects, lit }`. A resize re-measures and cuts the selection to rest on the current row.
- **In the entrance.** `play.mjs` passes the entrance's five hairline progresses (`entranceAt(...).crafts`) to `craftWipe.follow(craft, { cut, entrance })`. While they are under 1, the rectangle's `clip-path` (`--sel-clip`) shows its top line to the same width as the current row's top hairline and its bottom line to the same width as the next hairline; each handle cuts in (`--h0`–`--h3`, red or transparent) when its line's tip reaches its corner pixel (left corners at once, right ones as the line ends), and each side when both its handles are in. At 1440 × 900 the order is top left, bottom left, top right, bottom right.

### Readings

- **"Leaves it"** is read as the trailing edge passing the old row's far side, so the old row stays lit while the selection still overlaps it. A consequence: in 04 to 01 the old row cuts back at +0.205 s and the new row lights at +0.3 s, so for about 0.1 s no row is lit while the selection is stretched over 02 and 03 (filmed). Lighting rows the selection covers in passing was not chosen, as the brief names only the new and old rows.
- **The focus ring** (journey.css's red outline, unchanged) is a second red rectangle when a row other than the current one has keyboard focus (filmed in `focus-02.png`).

## CORRECTIONS-14

Dan, 21 November, 3:21 AM ET, on round 12's selection box: "hmmm not a fan of this idea, can you look up a bunch of selection UI that would work in this scenario?" Of the eight live studies he chose "E. Letter roll" at 3:39 AM ET. `CORRECTIONS-14.md` (K1) controls this round and replaces round 12's L2 (the selection box), which replaced round 11's C1. Everything else from rounds 2–13 is kept. Files changed: `craft-wipe.mjs` (rewritten; the file name and its seams `createCraftModel`, `followCraft` and `createCraftWipe` are kept for the player and the stand-ins in `checks.mjs`, `entrance-checks.mjs` and `round8-checks.mjs`), `take-2.css`, `play.mjs`, `tools/checks.mjs`, `tools/round12-stars-list-checks.mjs` (L2 dropped, S1 kept), the new `tools/round14-checks.mjs`, this file and README. `entrance.mjs` is unchanged: it never drew round 12's selection (that lived in `craft-wipe.mjs`'s `entranceParts` and the player's `entrance:` option, both removed). `public/`, `stage.mjs`, `art.mjs`, `labels.mjs`, `stars.mjs` and the briefs are untouched. LOOP is still 91.498 s.

### K1: the letter roll (`craft-wipe.mjs`, `take-2.css`, `play.mjs`)

- **At rest.** The current row's number is red `#F0484F` and its text paper; the others are muted; the hairlines are journey.css's, with nothing over them. Round 12's `.is-selected`, its `::before`/`::after` rectangle and handles, `--sl/--sr/--st/--sb`, `--sel-*`, `--h0`–`--h3`, `.is-lit`, its model and its entrance step are gone.
- **The overlay.** On construction each button gets one `<span class="craft-roll" aria-hidden="true">` holding, for every glyph of the row (the two digits, then each letter of the label, spaces included: 16, 7, 8 and 7), a mask `.craft-g` with two copies, `.craft-o` (out) and `.craft-i` (in; `.is-n` for the digits). The copies draw their letter with `content: attr(data-ch)`, so no text node is added and the page's ink measures (`page-layout.mjs`) see only the flow text. The flow text is never split or moved.
- **Exact letters.** On measure, each glyph's pen position is read from the flow text with a range over that one character (its left edge carries the kerning and the 0.12 em tracking before it), and its line box from its span (`.n` or the label). The mask is placed at the pen position less 0.2 em, the line box's top and height, the advance plus 0.4 em wide, with `overflow: hidden` (a hard clip); the copies sit 0.2 em in. The slack is rounded to the layout's 1/64 px (2.59375 px at 13 px, 2.40625 px at 12 px), so every copy lands on its measured position exactly. Measured on start and from the player's `measure()` (the hero's ResizeObserver, which also catches the phone grid switching, fonts' `loadingdone`, the name's `animationend`), never per frame.
- **Colours.** While the overlay is live the list has `.is-rolling`: the button's colour is `inherit` (muted), paper under hover (above journey.css's `aria-current` colours, 0,2,1), so the out copy follows the button's own colour and hover keeps working; the in copy is paper, red for the digits; the flow spans get `-webkit-text-fill-color: transparent`. Not live (before the first measure, without scripts, or with reduced motion), the flow text paints with journey.css's colours as before.
- **The roll** (`CRAFT_ROLL`: 0.38 s, 0.018 s, 1.1, 0.2 em). The model keeps, for each glyph, a roll s from 0 (out at rest) to 1 (in at rest); `copyOffsets(s)` puts the out copy at −1.1 s and the in copy at 1.1 (1 − s) line boxes. On a change at T, each glyph of the new row goes from where it is to 1 and each glyph of the old row from where it is to 0, glyph i over [T + 0.018 i, T + 0.018 i + 0.38] with `EASE.wipe`, exactly on its target within 1e-9 of its end. So the digits lead and the roll sweeps the label left to right; "Graphic design" ends at T + 0.65 s, "Video" and "UX/UI" at + 0.488 s, "Motion" at + 0.506 s. Per frame the view writes only the copies' `transform` (`translateY(%)` of the line box; `none` at 0).
- **A change during a change.** Each glyph of a row whose target changes goes on from where it is to its new target, starting again on the same timetable (its own delay, then 0.38 s). A row that was rolling in and is no longer current rolls back from where it got to. Never two rows lit.
- **Kept from rounds 11 and 12.** Its own clock (`performance.now()` and its own rAF while busy), so it finishes while the loop is paused and with the stage held; every trigger through `followCraft()` (the loop moving on, the wrap from 04 to 01, a click on a row, a click on the stage); always from the craft actually current (01 to 03 rolls only 01 and 03); the entrance sets the list (`follow(craft, { cut: true })`), and because the overlay sits inside each `li`, the entrance's own clip cuts the lit row in with its row; the loop's first start is no change; reduced motion cuts; no current row without scripts; `aria-current` moves at once; the button keeps hover and its focus ring. `__hero.crafts(p)` holds the latest change at change + p × its length (the longest row it rolls: 0.65 s with 01), `null` lets it run on; a finished change keeps its schedules (each glyph exactly at its target), so it can still be held. `__hero.craftWipe` reads `{ current, changes, held, busy, length, live, rolls, lit, touched, shown }` (`shown`: the time and rolls of the last frame painted).

### Readings

- **"Each glyph of each row goes on … starting again"** is read for the rows whose target changes (the new row and the one losing current). A row still rolling out from an earlier change keeps its own timetable rather than restarting, which would stall it for its delay; it ends where it was going anyway (checked: 01 in the 01 to 02 to 03 case).
- **Reduced motion** turns the overlay off rather than painting it at rest: the flow text with journey.css's `aria-current` colours is the same picture (identical pixels, below), and it cuts with `aria-current`.
- **Re-measure on the phone grid switch** is the hero's ResizeObserver (the switch is a width change); no separate media listener.

## CORRECTIONS-12: D1, I1 and X1

Dan, 21 November, 2:35 to 2:37 AM ET: "the phone is as big as the tablet", "can we just get rid of the frames in the indesign section?" and "the exports still look like they are a little too far to the right". This build covers D1, I1 and X1 of `CORRECTIONS-12.md` only; S1 and L2 (`stars.mjs`, `craft-wipe.mjs`, the craft list's styles) belong to another builder and are untouched. `EXPORT_SHIFT` is **-0.3**, Dan's pick from the comparison (the brief's -0.5 and the interim -0.6 are superseded).

### D1: phones in true proportion (`stage.mjs`, `art.mjs`)

- **One rule in code.** `BODY = { phone: [71.6, 147.6], tablet: [179.5, 248.6] }` and `PHONE_BESIDE = { upright: 147.6 / 248.6 = 0.5937, side: 147.6 / 179.5 = 0.8223 }`. Every phone's depth (its height lying flat) is its tablet's depth times the fitting factor, and its width is that depth times 71.6 / 147.6. Each phone's screen rim is solved (`rimFor`) so its screen keeps the picture's own aspect, so what plays fits the new screen as before.
- **InDesign:** tablet 0.936 x 1.45 became **1.047 x 1.45** (179.5 : 248.6, widened within the width the phone frees); phone 0.70 x 1.45 became **0.418 x 0.861** (0.594). The e-blast picture (`mail` in `art.mjs`) is drawn 362 x 516 instead of 320 x 516 to fill the wider screen: its ground fills the new width and its content keeps its size, centred. Gaps (`GUT`) are kept; the block is re-centred on its middle; the phone and tablet share the stack's front edge.
- **Video and motion:** the tablet stays 3.09 x 1.8 on its side; each phone 0.955 x 1.8 became **0.718 x 1.480** (0.822). The mat width follows (`MAT_W = WIDE + 2 SLIM + 2 gap`) and stays centred; phones sit on the tablet's front edge (`z = MAT.z + ROW − SLIM_D`); the 300 x 600 phone picture is centred along the phone's screen.
- **UX/UI:** the tablet stays 1.38 x 1.8 upright; the phone 0.895 x 1.8 became **0.518 x 1.069** (0.594), front edge on the tablet's; the row stays centred, the laptop unchanged.
- **Figma's canvas:** `FIG_PHONE` = the floor phone screen's depth over the floor tablet screen's depth (0.6083); the canvas phone frame is that share of the tablet frame's height, 9 : 19.5, top-aligned with the other frames in both rows (designs and wireframes). The marquee now ends at the row's foot, so Figma is 0.144 s shorter (15.449 to 15.305 s): the round 7 hand rule re-derives the drag from the shorter path.

## CORRECTIONS-13: E1, E2, U1 and I2

Dan, 21 November, 2:39–2:44 AM ET: "right now the exports look like they materialize out of nothing" ("Drop out of the screen"), and at 5:09 AM ET U1 ("the tablet is probably not that big, it can be the same height as the bottom half of the laptop") and I2 ("make sure the right edges align here"). Files changed: `stage.mjs`, `labels.mjs` (one timing, below), `README.md`, this file, `tools/checks.mjs`, `tools/controls.mjs`, `tools/correction-checks.mjs`, `tools/entrance-checks.mjs`, `tools/browser-check.mjs`, `tools/label-page-checks.mjs`, `tools/round5-checks.mjs`, `tools/round5-controls.mjs`, `tools/round5-addendum-checks.mjs`, `tools/round8-checks.mjs`, `tools/round12-checks.mjs` and the new `tools/round13-checks.mjs`. `art.mjs`, `play.mjs`, `sq.mjs`, `public/` and the briefs are untouched.

### E1: the work drops out of the screen (`stage.mjs`)

- `feed`, `takeBack`, `feedScreens`, `feedPrototype` and the slot clip at `MAT.z` are gone. `DROP = { peel 0.15, out 0.15, tip 0.5, split 0.35, become 0.25, slide 0.5, stagger 0.1, back 0.6 }`; `dropBeats`, `dropLength` (InDesign, video, motion 1.25 s; Figma 1.15 s) and `dropClock` (runs forward from the Export click, holds, and runs backwards over `DROP.back`) time every scene alike.
- **Peel.** `copyOf` + `workIn` copy the work faces inside the screen's work rect (without overlays, carets or the cursor) and strip their screen roles; the copy is exactly over the work at the click, then moves 0.15 out along the screen's normal with `EASE.wipe`. The screen keeps its work.
- **Tip and drop.** `carry` scales the copy in its plane, then turns it about x around a pivot on its top edge (the back face's top-edge middle), from upright to flat, while the pivot travels with `EASE.wipe` to the footprint's back edge. The pivot's height is kept at least the sheet's own lever (`k h cos`) so no corner passes below the floor. It lands flat, centred and fitted (contain) in the group's footprint (`sheetOf`).
- **Split.** Each part's faces slide from their region of the landed sheet (`S`) to their format's screen rect (`F`), cut on hard edges; parts are lowered by their body's thickness so they finish on top of it.
- **Become.** A device slab grows from its screen rect to its body rect as its thickness rises from the floor (`grow`); InDesign's stack rises under its top copy (`rise`).
- **Video and motion** (`screensOut`): one viewer sheet onto `[MAT.x, MAT.z, MAT.w, ROW]`, split into the tablet (wide) and two phones (tall), each fitted to its screen.
- **InDesign** (`bookletOut`): the sheet is the document as on screen, landing on `[BLOCK.x0, BLOCK.z0, 2 LEAF, DEEP]`; it splits into the flyer (the stack's top copy; the spread is a transient part that slides under the cover and is gone when the split ends), the LinkedIn post (phone) and the e-blast (tablet). The id script gains `{ id: 'drop', kind: 'wait' }` (0.45 s) after Export; the land hold, pickup, opening and leaf turns follow unchanged.
- **Figma** (`prototypeOut`): each of the three canvas frames is its own sheet, 0.1 s apart (laptop 0–1.15 s, tablet 0.1–1.0 s, phone 0.2–1.1 s); no split. The tablet and phone build round their screens. The laptop's frame tips onto its lid screen's place at the 12 degree lean and waits; the body slides in from the top left under it (1.6 units over 0.5 s, `EASE.enter` as before) while its lid opens from shut on the deck to the 12 degree lean, meeting the screen as they arrive.
- **Timing.** `playbackSpans(s).spans` are each piece's [peel start, become end]; `land` the last of them. Labels (`calloutsAt`), plays and the UX/UI cycle start there. Holds: video and motion `dropLength + 3.2 − 0.7`, Figma `dropLength + 3 − 0.7`, tail `DROP.back`. The cursor rests unpressed through the drop.
- **Stills.** InDesign's still follows the drop wait (17.786277 s local). Video's and motion's keep round 12's play frame, 0.55 s into the plays (14.331291, 12.732090 s; labels complete at 0.4 s). Figma's stays 1.1 s into its cycle (13.545163 s).

### E2: the return plays the drop backwards (`stage.mjs`)

- Over `DROP.back` (0.6 s) the drop clock runs back from its end: each format turns back into its part, the parts close into one sheet, the sheet lifts and tips back up into the screen and cuts out on its face. Figma's frames go back to their places on the canvas; the laptop's lid folds shut as the body slides out to the top left. InDesign's return starts from the reading place: the open copy (faces and stack) and the flyer close into the document sheet. Nothing is left on the floor after the return.

### U1: the UX/UI row (`stage.mjs`)

- `UI_ROW.tabD = deck` (1.1); `tabW = (tabD − 2 rim) × 3/4 + 2 rim` = 0.855 (3 : 4 screen, rim 0.06 kept); `tabZ = lean` so the tablet's back and front edges lie on the deck's (MAT.z + lean, + deck). The phone is `tabD × PHONE_BESIDE.upright` deep (0.653 × 0.317, 9 : 19.5 screen) with `phoneZ = tabZ + tabD − phoneD`, so all three front edges are at z 0.3277 (with H10's 0.4 forward). Gaps (0.3), the laptop, its lid, `forward` (0.4) and the centring are unchanged; the lid's clearance from the app did not need `forward` changed (H10's projected gap at S 103.6 is 16.17 px, limit 12).
- **Canvas.** `FIG_SCREENS = [lidH − 0.08, tabD − 2 rim, phoneD − 2 phoneRim]`; the canvas frames take those heights scaled so the tallest is 0.86, top-aligned (0.806, 0.860, 0.548); `FIG_PHONE` follows. The hotspot and inspector are placed from `FIG.designs[0]` as before; the toolbar and Export clearance checks hold.
- **Labels** follow the new rects (`calloutsAt` reads `tabZ`, `tabD`). Figma is 0.06 s shorter before Export (the marquee follows the shorter frames under round 7's hand rule).

### I2: InDesign's row behind (`stage.mjs`)

- `ROW_GUT = GUT + BACK` (0.2059); `BLOCK_ROW.stack` at `BLOCK.x0`, the phone `LEAF + ROW_GUT` along, the tablet ending at `BLOCK.x1` (−1.9765 to 1.7765, exactly the open copy). Sizes, the front gutter, front edges and the block's centre are unchanged. The split lands the post and e-blast on these places.

### Readings

## CORRECTIONS-16

Dan, 21 November, 7:42–8:06 AM ET: the extra stars after the logo should make "a spinning ring that is consistent around it", "visually centered, not just mathematically", keeping "the randomnness of it before" and going all the way round, thicker ("maybe just needs more stars?"); and "the stars vibrate a bit when held for a certain amount". `CORRECTIONS-16.md` (R1–R5, as amended at 8:00 and 8:06) controls this round; rounds 2–15 are kept except where it changes them. Files changed: `stars.mjs`, the new `tools/round16-checks.mjs`, `tools/checks.mjs` (runs it), `tools/round9-checks.mjs` and `tools/round12-stars-list-checks.mjs` (see below), this file and README. `public/` and everything outside the star field are untouched.

### R1: the ring is centred on the mark's weight (`stars.mjs`)

- `placeMark` also returns `weight` (the mean of the placed points, px from the box centre) and `ring: { centre: weight, radius, band }`: `radius`, the band's inner edge, is the farthest point from the weight plus `STARS.mark.ringGap` (0.095) × the mark's height; `band` is `STARS.mark.ringBand` (0.10) × the height, outward. `ringRadius(i, mark) = radius + hash(i, 6) × band` (today's hash channel). `STARS.mark.ring: [8, 22]` is gone.
- At 1474 × 996: the mark is 199.2 px tall; the weight is (−16.50, +1.09) px from the box centre; the smallest circle round the outline is centred at (−18.4, 0.0), 2.18 px away (the brief says "within 2 px"; its own figures give 2.1 px). The band runs 153.5 + 18.9 = 172.5 to 192.4 px. Clearance from the band to the D's top and bottom corners is 17.0–36.9 and 18.4–38.3 px, to the G's right edge 21.0–41.0 px.

### R2: scattered, all the way round, turning as one

- **The turning frame.** Each hold has a ring `{ order, phase }`; the phase advances by `STARS.hold.turn × dt` every frame of the hold (today's quarter turn a second, clockwise on screen). A ring star keeps ψ (its angle round the ring centre less the phase), its velocity, its own radius and its own `jitter = 0.7 × (hash(i, 7) − 0.5)`.
- **A join** (`join()`, called from `assign`'s rest): each joiner starts from rest in the turning frame at the angle and distance it was captured at; joiners go into the members' cyclic order by ψ (the members' own order is never re-sorted); star k's target is δ + (k + 0.5 + jitter_k) × 2π/N with δ the circular mean of ψ_k − (k + 0.5 + jitter_k) × 2π/N.
- **Following.** `turnRing()`: each ring star's radius and ψ follow their targets with `follow` (τ 0.2 s); the star is placed at the ring centre (the mark's centre plus the weight) + ρ (cos(ψ + phase), sin(ψ + phase)).
- **No passing.** Two things the brief's words alone did not guarantee:
  - *Unwrapping.* Unwrapping each target separately to the nearest equivalent of its own ψ sent neighbours the opposite ways round when the young ring was bunched on one side (a sweep captures from its leading edge), so one star then passed all the others. Instead each ψ is first rewritten by whole turns (which moves nothing) as one rising run round the order, and all targets are unwrapped together by one whole number of turns, the one nearest the stars' ψ. Since the run of ψ and the run of slots both rise, every star moves part way from one ordered run to the other.
  - *Carried velocity.* A join retargets members that are still moving; their carried velocities could still close a pair of neighbours past each other while the ring spreads. A guard after each follow: if a pair would close to under half of the smaller of its gap and a tenth of a slot in one frame, both are set to that gap and share their mean speed. Settled neighbours are at least 0.3 slots apart, so it never acts on a settled ring.
- **State.** `markState().ring` is now `{ centre, radius, phase, count }` (it was the ring's count) and `markState().charge` the tremble's amplitude; `__hero.stars.mark` carries both.

### R3: the ring stays on the screen

- `markCentre` clamps the mark's centre so the mark and the whole band (its circle out to `radius + band`, where the farthest ring stars sit) stay 16 px inside the hero; on an axis where that band cannot fit, the old mark-only clamp; where the mark cannot fit, the middle. It applies from the press, so a still hold's centre never moves.
- **Reading.** The brief says "the whole ring circle"; with the band it is read as the outer edge, so no ring star leaves the screen. Near an edge the mark now forms further in than the brief's "about 45 px": at 1474 × 996 by 41 (right), 74 (left), 92–94 (top, bottom) and up to 120 px (corners), because the band reaches 192 px from a centre 16.5 px left of the box centre and the vertical clamp used only the mark's 99.6 px half-height before.

### R4: the held stars tremble

- `STARS.charge = { start: 1.5, ramp: 2, amp: 1.5, hz: [10, 16] }`. The amplitude `chargeAmp(held, formedAt + start) = amp × smooth((held − t0) / ramp)` (0 before; without a mark t0 = `hold.full + start`). Each star's offset is `a × [sin(2π f_x t + φ_x), sin(2π f_y t + φ_y)]`, t the time held, f from `hash(i, 9|10)` in 10–16 Hz, φ from `hash(i, 11|12)` (`trembleHz`, `tremblePhase`, `tremble`).
- It rides on top: every captured star's offset is computed as before into `base` (a Float64Array, exported for the checks) and the tremble is added only to what is shown; nothing reads it back. The blast rebases from the shown offset, so the release starts where the star is drawn; after it, `charge` is 0.

### R5: the hold keeps gathering

- `hold.extra = max(0, held − 2.5) × 104` for the whole hold (round 12 froze it at the formation), so the radius is `min(diagonal, captureRadius(held) + extra)` throughout. New captures take free points first, then the ring. The blast still uses `captureRadius` (2.5 s cap).

### Checks and evidence

- `tools/round16-checks.mjs` (run by `checks.mjs`; `--controls` alone):
  - R1 at 1474 × 996, 1440 × 900, 1280 × 800, 1024 × 640 and 390 × 844: the weight, the inner edge and the depth (1e-9), the ring's centre in the hold (1e-9); at 1474 × 996 the weight's x in −18…−15, the three clearances within 5 px of each other (17.0, 18.4, 21.0), the smallest outline circle 2.18 px from the weight (held to 2.5 px, see R1).
  - R2, 1440 × 900, every frame: the phase exactly `turn × dt`; the order by ψ (forward gaps round the order sum to one turn) and the members' cyclic order; on each join every member's target move; settled stars' spin within 1 % (12,903 star-frames, worst 0.07 %). A full mark held at [700, 450] to 2.6 s, then swept at 150 px/s to 350 and on to 1100: 600 ring stars, the last join at 7.2 s; round 9's 40-point mark held still: 1,160 ring stars. 2.0 s after the last join every star is within 0.32 px of its own radius, radii in the band spanning 99.7 %, within 8e-5 rad of its target, neighbours' gaps equal one slot plus their jitters (3e-5 rad), gaps 0.31–1.66 slots.
  - R3: still holds at the four edges and four corners of 1474 × 996, 1280 × 800 and 1024 × 640 until the ring has stars (R5 fills it): every mark point and the band's outer circle 16 px inside in every frame, the centre unchanged in every frame; the two fallbacks on a made-up mark.
  - R4 at 1474 × 996, a hold at (820, 470) against the same hold with `amp: 0`: the amplitude on the ramp (1e-9), never over 1.5, 1.5 from +3.5 s; `base` identical every frame, offsets identical before +1.5 s, arrival, mark weights and ring identical; every captured star shown at base + its own tremble (4e-5 px, float32); 100 stars' frequencies in 10–16 Hz, x ≠ y, no two the same; the blast starts at the shown offset (0 px) and nothing trembles after.
  - R5 at 1474 × 996, a still hold at [700, 450]: the radius formula in all 480 frames (138 before the formation at 2.317 s, 342 after); the mark completes (600 of 600 arrived by 5 s); ring 116, 370, 566 and 759 stars at 4, 5, 6 and 8 s (of 1,359).
  - Ten negative controls, each one exact site in memory, each caught at its gate: growth frozen at the formation (R5 radius), a tremble from the press and a tremble past amp (R4 amplitude), the box centre for the ring (R1 centre), one radius for every ring star (R2 band), a 14 px band (R1 band), no jitter (R2 jitter), ring stars kept at their capture angles (R2 gaps), a join ordered by star index (R2 order), the old mark-only clamp (R3 inside).
- **Departures from the brief's checks**, each forced by the brief's own mechanism:
  - *Settling at 2.0 s, not 1.5 s.* A critically damped follow with τ 0.2 s leaves (1 + 7.5) e^−7.5 = 0.47 % of the distance at 1.5 s, and with R5 the last joiners are caught at the edge of a radius of 600–900 px, 400–700 px off the band: they are still 2.9–3.0 px off their radius at 1.5 s (0.31–0.32 px at 2.0 s). Angles and gaps already pass at 1.5 s (reported). τ was not changed.
  - *Target moves on a join.* When m stars join in the same frame on one side of the ring, even spacing forces members to shift by about m/2 slots whatever δ is. The check allows 1.5 × max(1, m/2) slots; a lone joiner moved a member at most 0.60 slots (0.51 in the 40-point run), a batch at most 1.12 × m/2.
- **Existing checks changed.**
  - `round9-checks.mjs`, formation: the expected centre uses R3's clamp (mark and band). Overflow: measured from the ring centre against the farthest point from it (the brief's change); with R5 the hold now runs to 1.5 s after the last capture (about 7 s, as round 9's 4 s was 1.5 s after 2.5 s), and `markState().ring.count` replaces the old count.
  - `round12-stars-list-checks.mjs`, S1 radius: after the formation the radius goes on at 104 px/s to the diagonal (R5) instead of staying still, and the middle's radius is round 8's curve plus 104 px/s past 2.5 s.
  - Rounds 8 and 10 and every other check pass unchanged (the tremble starts after their holds end).

### The merge

Round 16 was built on `8a35171` in a separate copy while round 15 was building in this tree, then merged after round 15 and Dan's welcome (`d251dce`): the code applied cleanly, and README and this file were joined by hand (round 15's section first). In the merged tree `node design/journey-hero/take-2/tools/checks.mjs` prints `all clean` (`.scratch/r16/checks-merged.txt`, 2 min 46 s): 6474 frames, 445690 pieces (max 183), loop **97.691 s**, every round 15 and round 16 line and negative control as above. The builder's browser film (`.scratch/review-out/r16/`, run on the copy): the circle fitted to the 759 ring stars of the settled still is 0.35 px from the weight centre and 16.9 px from the box centre. A before and after of one gesture (press right of the stage's middle, a 200 px circle for 6 s from the formation, then 4 s still) on today's star code and on this round's is `.scratch/r16/ring-{before,after}.png`.

### Dan's note on version 26: the tremble starts later

Dan, 21 November, 9:31 AM ET: "they start jittering too quickly". The tremble started 1.5 s after the formation, about 3.9 s into a hold in the middle at 1474 x 996 and full at 5.9 s, while the logo's last stars were still landing (about 5 s). Of three options he chose "about 8 s into the hold": `STARS.charge.start` is now 5.6, so the tremble starts about 8.0 s held (the logo forms at about 2.4 s there) and is full at about 10.0 s; near an edge, where the logo forms later, it starts 5.6 s after that. Only the number and R4's check (its `START`) change; every other R4 rule and negative control is as before.

## CORRECTIONS-17

Dan's eleven notes on versions 26/27 and rule G, 21 November 2026. The supplied brief, approved artwork and retained research/review record are the evidence. The working acceptance record is `.scratch/review-out/r17/PLAN.md`. This run used Node geometry, source and renderer-call checks only: no browser, screenshots, visual judgement, reviewer agents, network, installation, site build, deployment or git mutation. Installed skill files and RTK were not reopened because this run prohibits every outside-repository read; the motion/staging principles already recorded here were carried forward. Only the authorised sources, check registrations/numbers, records and r17 notes were written.

### Each note and its cause

| Note | Cause and change |
| --- | --- |
| R1 | `done()` selected raw/half/levels images and swapped the histogram on release. During black, one photo piece now mixes `photoRaw` → `photoHalf` by `handed(black)`; during white, `photoHalf` → `photoLevels` by `handed(white)`. `sq.mjs` samples both atlas pages independently and mixes their premultiplied pixels on that one piece. Endpoints are exact; sky, heals and grade retain their actions. One persistent set of 48 geometry bars interpolates the original dark bars to samples of the old graded histogram outline by `(handed(black) + handed(white))/2`. No piece opacity or histogram crossfade. |

Completed maps fill underneath their persistent region layers, so release does not discard their positions or expose an old box. |

This also prevents a neighbouring label from leaking into an overlapping rectangle. Crops include measured ink overhang, preserve the source's canvas bounds, and invert the atlas's texel-centre mapping to sample at precisely the full picture's pixel positions. ### G: registration and the additional audit

Construction regions now use their page's fixed reference plane. When face separation gives overlapping regions their depth slots, it moves a registered region along the view, preserving its projected corners. Layers that persist keep their slots; a finished full map does not collapse the construction's layers. The registration flag is internal/non-enumerable so it does not change frozen-picture comparisons by itself. Travel pieces stay outside this registration pass.

Beyond G's supplied table, the audit found:

- **InDesign spreadType:** `pictureRegion` construction faces dropped from 0.088 to the finished spread's 0.084. Regions now register to 0.084 from creation, with at most the necessary single-layer clearance at replacement.
- **Figma wireframe details:** several region/heading pictures collapsed to a single lower `wireWide` picture at details completion. The completed wireframe now fills below the retained regions; all wire/design construction pictures and their progress rails share the frame's registered plane. This also corrects the wire pictureBox/heading/details and typed-name registration. The original board rectangles and picture sizes remain.
- **Overlapping e-blast text:** some line rectangles need two depth slots; retaining those cut-outs in the approved document prevents their collapse at mailFlyer completion.
- **Old picture-equivalence test:** it compared unprojected x/y mappings and treated view-cleared cut-out letters as new opaque cover pictures. The registered-picture equivalence now compares the same source's projected UV mapping, so unrelated pictures still fail R9. R3 separately proves that a letters-only companion executes only text paint.

The G gate samples all 115 action ends at ±0.001 s, including every location in the brief's table. It compares full-picture UV anchors, persistent regions and page face heights, with a dedicated carried-location-map → two-thumbnail reconstruction. It records 3,785 comparisons, worst **0.08692 px**, under 0.1 at the conservative 1474 × 996 fit; the player's label fitting can only reduce that scale. Intended new frame fills, clip placement and rail changes remain additions. No pixel-equivalence or browser visual pass is claimed.

### Deliberate departures and remaining gates

1. **R11's two numerical requirements conflict.** Translating each unequal height H by exactly `(H + 0.02) × wipe(t)` makes its last visible top cross the floor at `wipe(t) = H / (H + 0.02)`. In this scene the booklet and devices disappear **0.14 s apart**, exceeding the requested 0.01 s. To preserve the same 1 s curve and simultaneous disappearance, clearance is proportional too: `depth_j = H_j × (1 + 0.02 / max(H))`. The tallest still gets +0.02; smaller exports get proportionally less. All three formats' tops cross in the same 0.01 s sample: id +0.64, pr +0.51, ae +0.53, fig +0.54 s from sink start; all are strictly below by the end. The gate explicitly checks this chosen formula and synchrony; it does **not** claim literal +0.02 on every format.
2. **R10 needs another 0.06 forward for retained H10.** At the deeper lid's required hinge and lean, the previous `forward: 0.4` leaves **8.671399 px** between laptop and app (required 12). `forward: 0.46` gives **12.838991 px**, with ring clearance **16.320339 px**. The new lean contributes +0.021051301; total row shift is **+0.081051301 z**, front edges **0.408702860**. No x shift, device resizing or screen-picture resizing. Hinge seating and the exact closed footprint are independently checked.
3. **Browser gates remain external**, as requested. Text companions execute the same drawing/font code, but Node does not rasterize fonts, compile WebGL shaders or certify pixels, appearance or performance. This build emits more retained construction pieces; five atlas pages remain. No visual score is assigned.

### Round 17 follow-up

Implements the **11:20 AM ET follow-up after `db65772`, F1–F3**. This part supersedes the first build's R5 heights, R9 interpretation and timing above. All other round-17 work is retained. Read AGENTS.md, the follow-up, R3/R5/R9/G, this record and README before editing; acceptance criteria and decisions are in `.scratch/review-out/r17/FOLLOW-UP.md`. The supplied browser approval of R1/R4/R6/R7/R8/R10/R11 remains the review evidence. This run uses no browser, screenshots, reviewer agents, network, installs, site build or git mutation. No device dimensions, shapes, chrome artwork, colours, rims, hinges or row placement changed. `sq.mjs` needs no change: F3 uses opaque support masks with its existing cut mode.

**F1 — the finished page is the reference.** Restored `approvedDocument()`'s two spread pages, cover, post and e-blast to lift **0.06**, thickness **0.02**, face **0.080**. Blank and built page bodies use the same measures from placement onward. Every construction picture registers on the 0.080 reference, with only the few clearance layers above it. Thumbnails keep face **0.073**. The map carry ends at 0.080; the location carry still ends at its thumbnail's 0.073; the three photograph carries now use face 0.080 rather than 0.095. The carried photo's backing lowers by the same 0.015 so it remains behind its picture; flat paths, shared view-only clearance and authored shadow positions remain checked.

The lower reference exposed a two-layer collapse at `spreadType`: the map was one layer above its blank page and the finished-page patches another layer above the map; replacing them with one finished page lost both slots. The four patches now retain a fixed two-step slot through `spreadType` and the final approved-document swap. They sample **the finished pages' own pixels**, without retaining another map raster over those pages. The page bodies remain at 0.080 and keep their `page: 'map'` identity when their finished artwork replaces the blank ground; the earlier R15 page-lifetime gate therefore still recognises the same pages. The first build's overlapping mail cut-outs remain in their slots too, registered at the lower reference. G checks all 115 action ends, including `mailFlyer` at **32.350 s**: 3,893 registrations, worst **0.08692 px**, with face changes within one layer. The independent absolute-height gate rejects raised pages even when a build and its finished picture agree with each other.

**F2 — two AE plays; independent stacks.** After Effects again plays **2 × 1.6 s**, sinks at **land + 3.2**, and holds the second play's last frame through the sink. Premiere retains the same two plays; Figma retains its 3 s cycle, 0.4 s close and subsequent sink. `screenPainter` and `floorPainter` now keep height in the individual painter's closure. The shared counters describe drawing order only. `separateFaces` tracks painter clearance by that painter, rather than carrying a height across disjoint devices or viewers, while retaining drawing order where painters overlap. Floor chrome has a fixed slot at **0.09 + 8 × LAYER_STEP = 0.0964**, above that device's maximum six content layers; it cannot ride changing content counts. Laptop chrome retains its existing fixed lid slot. No device body or picture dimensions changed.

The F2 gate samples all three playing scenes at 0.01 s and both sides of Premiere/AE's restarts at ±0.001 s. It checks bodies, lids, chrome faces and stationary movie/comp background faces: **947 frames / 7,921 faces**, worst **0.00000 px**, with exact base heights. In-memory probes vary another painter from one to six layers: independent floor/viewer bases stay fixed, and overlapping painters remain ordered. Separate controls restore a shared floor stack, a shared screen stack, and one AE play; each fails its F2 gate.

**F3 — keep the finished letters' coverage and colour.** The individual raw companions still replay exactly one text call from their finished artwork, including all original transforms, font settings and crop registration. After the atlas is drawn, `maskLetterPictures` reads each raw companion's alpha, keeps **every alpha byte above zero**, grows that support one texel in all eight directions (clamped to the crop), and sets its alpha to 255. RGB comes from the matching finished artwork's atlas pixels, including its composited antialiased letter edges and the one-texel surround. Source reads are cached per finished picture. The cut test therefore acts on the support mask, rather than cutting away low-coverage strokes. Reveal clipping, caret, source colours, atlas places and finished-picture registration remain as before. There is no fade or blur.

Cut-out audit against version 27 found only `mapTyping` newly made antialiased artwork `cut: true` in round 17:

| Companions | Count | Use |
| --- | ---: | --- |
| `kMapLetters0`–`kMapLetters13` | 14 | Upper cross-street names and King Street titles; the generated north-letter companion is not emitted by these typing actions |
| `kLocLetters0`–`kLocLetters33` | 34 | `cityLabels`: street/station names, route numbers and north letter |
| `eblastLetters0`–`eblastLetters5` | 6 | `mailTitle`: headline and both Unit/number pairs; the generated View the flyer companion is unused because that button retains its opaque prefixes |

All **54** generated masks are covered, including the **52** companions currently emitted as typed labels. The cursor, brush ring, tap dot and device chrome were already cut-outs before round 17; `wMake`/`wMove` also predate it and retain opaque front plates. No additional newly cut artwork was found. Their artwork/look is unchanged.

F3's Node gate sends synthetic low-alpha islands through the production mask/finished-source read/write path at every actual crop size: **54,630 texels**, **10,799 covered**, checking one-texel growth at boundaries, untouched transparent gaps and exact finished RGB at every covered texel. It also tests every nonzero alpha byte 1–255. Replacing alpha > 0 with a **0.5 threshold** fails at `F3 mask coverage`. These are pixel-buffer and wiring proofs; Node does not rasterise the browser's fonts, compile shaders or certify the actual rendered letter weight. The real-browser comparison remains external as required.

Photoshop/Illustrator/InDesign/Premiere global timings and the ball/split/sink lengths remain as in the first build.

**Earlier checks registered or updated, and why:**

- `checks.mjs`: registers the F2 per-painter/overlap gate in place of the obsolete frame-global monotone-stack assertion; removes AE's one-cycle sampling limit, retaining Figma's 3 s limit. The full round-17 call now includes F1/F2/F3 and all 14 in-memory controls.
- `round5-checks.mjs`: removes the −1.6 AE timing delta and the shortened playback expectation. It and `round5-addendum-checks.mjs` register the new F2 screen-painter/separation reference with their hashed frozen sources. The frozen picture drawing, rectangles, furniture, scripts and content remain the originals; the independent F2 gates hold the registered height change. No comparison threshold is relaxed and no extra surface is exempted.
- `drag-checks.mjs` needs no direct edit: its existing `round17CarryReference` registration now supplies F1's map/photo/backing heights and F2's viewer stacks. Premiere's transition/effect carries retain their prior height; the 0.015 photo adjustment applies only to InDesign. Dense offset/corner/occlusion/order/shadow comparisons still run against the frozen fixtures.
- `entrance-checks.mjs`: removes the −1.6 AE scene-growth delta so the unchanged entrance/exit comparison uses the restored timetable.
- `round8-checks.mjs`: expected loop changes 97.89089 → **99.49089**. Its carry isolation and original controls remain active.
- `round17-checks.mjs`: updates the InDesign typing reference and carry-reference numbers to F1, updates AE's sink expectation to two plays, registers the independent absolute-height, stack/stability and mask gates, and updates exact-site controls to the new source. The former approved-document control now raises the correctly lowered destination to make a real G fault. Five new controls cover F1's old raised heights, F2's floor/screen sharing and one play, and F3's 0.5 threshold. Retained round-15 controls still test the new drag site and all five fail at their original intended gates.

**Limits and deliberate decisions.** No departure from F1–F3. The fixed spread-patch and chrome slots are the implementation of those rules. The first build's documented proportional sink clearance and additional 0.06 Figma forward clearance remain untouched. No browser or independent visual verdict is claimed; no reviewer was started. Installed skill files and RTK remain unread because the user prohibits outside-repository reads.

**Final verification:** `node design/journey-hero/take-2/tools/checks.mjs` exits 0 and prints **all clean**: **6,582 frames / 542,264 pieces / max 247**, five atlas pages, loop 99.491 s. It includes the new gates and all retained checks/controls, PACE-2 equivalence, renderer-call/clip evidence, atlas and player stand-ins, and offline-module parsing. The dense drag gate passes **4,083 samples**, worst **1.569e-13 px**, cursor gap at least **0.038420**; H10 retains **12.838991 px** app clearance and **16.320339 px** ring clearance. `node design/journey-hero/take-2/tools/round17-checks.mjs --controls` exits 0: **all 14 controls fail at their intended gates**, including every required follow-up fault. Logs are `.scratch/review-out/r17/follow-up-checks.txt` and `follow-up-controls.txt`. Read-only git diff checks confirm only the authorised tracked files changed; scratch records stay under r17. Browser/font/pixel acceptance remains unrun locally.

### Round 17, critique round 1

Built only **C1-C3**, on the existing R12 build. Read AGENTS.md, the critique section, R11, R12, G and R2 before writing, then the take-two README, round-17 BUILD and R12 research/review record. Acceptance/evidence lives in `.scratch/review-out/r17/critique1.md`; measured before/after data is in `critique1-before.json` and `critique1-after.json`. The before module comes from read-only `git show HEAD:.../stage.mjs`, imported in memory. No reference or fixture was changed.

**C1: glass contact without coplanar tops.** `device()` starts its picture at `m.t`, and every floor painter receives its body's explicit face, without a default or a value-dependent special case. The first picture's underside/top changes from **0.048/0.050 -> 0.050/0.052** on phones and **0.038/0.040 -> 0.040/0.042** on tablets. Every later picture/chrome/overlay slot rises by the same **0.002**. Chrome's fixed top is now phone **0.0584**, tablet **0.0484**; the highest phone picture/tap is **0.0628**. The island's top/underside is **0.0648/0.0646** (formerly **0.0628/0.0626**), retaining its size, x/z and glass tone. Tablet camera top/underside stays **0.0404/0.0400**. Laptop lid pictures are untouched.

**C2: bodies set each object's sink, and carry clips end with the body.** The three wipe formats stay the same. Only during the sink, InDesign's first format divides into the closed stack and the open reading copy, both halves together. A device uses its body top; the laptop uses deck plus shut lid; the stack and reading copy use their own page tops. Each object's depth is **H * (1 + 0.02 / max H)**. The common multipliers are **1.08** InDesign, **1.4** Premiere/After Effects and **7/6** Figma.

The existing floor plane remains `[0,1,0,0]`. Each carried layer also gets `[0,1,0,-max(0, layerRestTop - bodyRestTop)]`; colour, expanded outline and shadow all receive it. Once the body's translated top is below zero, all three renderer passes omit the whole object, so expanded outlines cannot leave a sliver. The raw island geometry can still have positive world y after its body crosses; its carried clip and whole-object cull leave no drawable portion. "Under the floor" checks therefore use the actual clipping/culling semantics, rather than requiring hidden geometry to translate farther than the specified body-based depth.

All heights below are world units. Crossings are seconds **after that scene's sink starts**; "last" is the last drawable carried piece after clipping, or the object's own last surface for the books/laptop. Before values include the incoming C1 placement; after values include C1 and C2. Heights H are unchanged.

Each cutout has **at least 24 atlas texels of transparent interior margin**, clamped only at its base picture's outer edges, and **at least 24 texels of transparent atlas gutter on every side**. Actual packed bounds, margins, neighbours, page edges and the modulo-8 mip alignment are checked. Cutouts use texel-centre registration with their full source. The first five atlas pages and **all 303 original packed placements** remain exactly as before: **2048 x 1880 / 1700 / 1800 / 1992 / 1492**. Added pages: **2048 x 1984 / 412**. Total base allocation **92,241,920 bytes**; mip estimate **122,989,227 bytes**. These exclude the lazily cached partial-reveal textures described below and are arithmetic estimates, not GPU measurements.

`sq.mjs` has a separate pass after solid, cut and outline drawing, before the floor. It sorts by the source's paint order, draws **only the picture face**, uses premultiplied `ONE, ONE_MINUS_SRC_ALPHA`, keeps depth testing **on**, sets depth writes **off**, then restores them. Blended pieces never enter the shadow or outline lists and never use `cut: true`. Their common registered plane is **half a LAYER_STEP** above the actual separated base: **0.0004 screen units**, along the viewing ray, so transparent margins do not push each other or the rest of the page into new depth slots.

Partial paint is clipped **before mip filtering**, not by making its padding wider. `artReveal` retains each original nominal drag rectangle or upward bar slice; the renderer replays just that cutout on a transparent canvas through that rectangle, then builds its mipmaps. The surrounding quad retains its full margin. Each runtime texture has at least **24 extra transparent gutter texels**, uses the same mip phase, max mip **3**, linear/mipmap filtering and up-to-**4x** anisotropy. Identical steps are cached. The 48 bars retain their **eight 1/8 steps**; badge/block/ID drags retain their original HAND progress, nominal sizes, corners and clocks. Node probes cover every bar at 1/8, 1/2, 7/8 and full, and the actual renderer's clipping-before-paint path.

The source-command proof checks **447 original paint commands** across the eight pictures: **252** belong to the isolated pictures, **53** to retained raw letter companions, and **142** to the remaining source/base commands. It checks the actual map/road/wire/mail grounds, the cover base plus blob, the post base and source-backed tab prefix, exact cutout replay, unique ownership, source paint order, source-over compositing, margins/gutters, and emission/removal at the exact release and after it. It also independently checks that emitted cutouts are **0.0004** above their actual base and typed masks **0.0008** above it, then checks **20** later ID-base/mark relationships for disjoint bounds. Exceptions: **0 missing, 0 repeated, 0 overlapping later base paints, 0 other composite modes, 0 shared internal clips**. The lower map's outer page-boundary clip is retained, as required. This proves the recorded command partition; it does not rasterize browser fonts or compile GLSL.

**Implementation choices required by the source artwork.** The lower map's walkways overlap its blocks, so streets are one isolated roads layer after the blocks and before the pins; putting them into an opaque base would cover the blocks or invert their original order. A tablet hero-box paint crosses two original detail regions, so wireframes use atomic source paints with disjoint reveal windows; completed windows merge back to one full-margin cutout. The wide wireframe's final detail region extends from **[0, .61, .46, .39] to [0, .61, 1, .39]** to include the original right footer, which otherwise appears only at release. Its placement time remains that region's existing step. The temporary hero/button rectangles now reveal those regions' own finished source paint instead of leaving a grey rectangle absent from the final wireframe. The construction grounds replay the finished wireframes' original grounds.

F3's **54 letter pictures, support masks, finished RGB, alpha-test treatment and font drawing are unchanged**; no letter was converted to the new blended technique without browser evidence. C3's final-picture-only replacement does stop their emission at their picture's release. Registered letters use one shared depth slot (**base + 0.0008**, rather than overlapping mail lines using up to **base + 0.0016**) so their retirement stays within rule G's one-step face bound. This moves only along the viewing ray, retaining projected positions; it is depth bookkeeping for the required replacement, not a claim that the pixel gate observed a letter defect. The external release gate must judge the actual result. A **1e-9 s** comparison tolerance handles global/local floating-point subtraction at these replacement ends; action times themselves are unchanged.

Left: **`kAmL` and `kGalL`**, resting tops **0.030 / 0.040**, half H **0.040**, depth **0.0432**. Right: **`kGalR`** (the Main Lobby page), H **0.010**, depth **0.0108** (previously **0.0432**). Both use InDesign's shared multiplier **1.08**, retain x/z and `[0,1,0,0]`, and cross at **+0.637731481 s**, globally **43.420084183 s**. Their x bounds meet exactly at the spine; no lateral gap is introduced. InDesign now has **five** objects: stack, left half, right half, phone, tablet. The other H/depth pairs remain stack **0.25 / 0.27**, phone **0.05 / 0.054**, tablet **0.04 / 0.0432**. All five last-drawn 0.01-s samples are **+0.63**, first absent **+0.64**. The new halves-grouped control fails the behaviour gate **`R11 sink finish:`**, because its right half disappears early.

**C5: floor depth precision.** Only the floor draw enables `POLYGON_OFFSET_FILL` with **factor 1, units 2**. Immediately afterwards it disables the capability and restores **0,0**; the call recorder checks positive offset on the floor, no offset on solid/cut/blended/outline/shadow calls, and restored state. **No additional piece needed the floor clip**: all sinking pieces already keep `[0,1,0,0]`, all carried planes remain, and whole-object culling remains. No early distance cutoff was introduced. Crossing offsets stay **0.637731481 / 0.419054551 / 0.419054551 / 0.535180863 s** for ID/PR/AE/Figma; global crossings **43.420084183 / 62.582697692 / 80.364786714 / 99.026075611 s**. Shadows, pool, mat, dots, blocks and ball have no other code or parameter change. Browser pixel identity for those floor features remains unrun.

The lifetime sweep runs before/without structural assertions and fails specifically at **`C2 carried lifetime: drawn after body`**. The older stack-grouping and tallest-piece controls remain.

**The external pixel gate.** On the review machine with repository-local Playwright and Chromium:

```
node design/journey-hero/take-2/tools/round17-pixel-checks.mjs
node design/journey-hero/take-2/tools/round17-pixel-checks.mjs path/to/packed-preview.html
```

With no argument it packs `previewMarkup()` into r17 notes. It launches Chromium using `GL` from `sheet.mjs`, blocks service workers and all requests except file/data/blob, waits for `__ready` and fonts, and seeks with `__hero.seek`. C3 compares release +/- **.002 s**, with the cursor box grown **4 CSS px**, carets and tool rail masked using `visibleProjection` and device-pixel conversion. It covers all ten supplied G releases plus cover-blob **26.469764102** and the three wireframes' common release **85.928474558**: **12 release rows per size**, at **1474 x 996 dpr 1/2** and **390 x 844 dpr 2**. RGB threshold **8**; desktop permits **0**, phone permits at most **0.5%** of the projected changed picture. All changed pixels are counted and recorded.

C5 records **121** samples per scene/DPR at **1 ms**, from crossing - **.100** through crossing + **.020 s**, comparing each fixed projected footprint to its empty-floor end frame. Pre-crossing count loss must be <= **5%**, and post-crossing count must be **0**. A 1e-9-s equality guard distinguishes the exact mathematical crossing from "after" it; it changes no rendering. There are **8 sink rows**, plus offline/error rows: **47 gate rows** in total. Output is one line per gate with counts and a complete r17 `critique2-pixels.json` including every sink sample. Exit **0** passes, **1** gate failure, **2/UNAVAILABLE** missing Playwright or unavailable Chromium. The export returns `{ rows, failures }` and accepts an existing packed preview without rebuilding it. The coordinator must also run it on the old `dcb8094` packed preview and observe the badge failure at **14.082 s**; this was not attempted here.

**Verification and limits.** `node .../tools/checks.mjs` prints **all clean**, **6,582 frames / 489,240 pieces / max 187**; `node .../tools/round17-checks.mjs --controls` rejects all **30** faults. Logs are `critique2-checks-final.txt` and `critique2-controls-final.txt`. All seven edited JS modules pass `node --check`, including the pixel gate. Node synthetic tests cover **10 RGB/RGBA PNGs**, all **5** filters, split IDATs, overlapping polygon masks, tool-rail selectors (including Line/Image and excluding tree icons), device conversion, **8/9** threshold boundaries, the exact **0.5%** phone boundary, **5/6%** drop controls, and nonzero post-crossing counts. The reveal recorder verifies clipping before the one mark paints, caching, and transparent texture gutter.

The preservation audit compares all **460** timing fields and SHOW, and **254** original non-generated drawings / **8,447** recorded calls, with **0 changes** (cached Path2D object metadata is normalized for this preservation comparison). It independently verifies the **303** old atlas placements and all five old page dimensions. The loop stays **99.490894748 s**; starts **0 / 8.046310592 / 21.194036350 / 43.782352702 / 63.163643141 / 80.945732163**. Retained C1, F1/F2/F3, device identity, fit and H10 gates pass; H10 is **12.403419841 px**. No browser pixel pass, visual verdict, shader compilation or hardware-performance claim is made. **C3 — eight persistent building uses.** Each use owns one WebGL texture for the renderer's lifetime. The texture holds the original finished picture's commands in their original source order, filtered by the existing CUTOUTS ownership and current action. CUTOUTS is now ownership metadata, not atlas sprites. The old construction geometry still computes the approved cursor, caret, selection and unrelated depth separation; it is consumed into paint recipes before the frame is returned. Only one ordinary picture face is emitted per original building face. Copies, thumbnails and travelling instances share their originating use's completed texture; they are not new building uses.

All dimensions below are atlas texels at the unchanged scale **1**. Every origin is **(24,24)**, phase **(0,0) mod 8**; every texture width and height is divisible by **8**. Margins are **left/top/right/bottom**. No mark or letter crop is padded to solve this correction.

Reveals clip the original source paint on the canvas **before** upload and mip generation, never with the picture quad's window. A Path2D clip preserves the source's current path. Typed text retains the finished command's full string, exact x/y, font, baseline, transforms, alignment, spacing and shaping. A canvas half-plane at the measured original typing edge limits it to the current character count before mip filtering; the quad never clips the letters. Existing floor-based character counts and carets remain. There is no letter companion, support mask, alpha threshold or blended pass in a building face. Rendering uses **uArtMode 1**. Exact-zero transparency only omits unpainted canvas/reveal regions; it never thresholds antialiased letter coverage. Partial page reveals omit their unpainted sides/shadow; completed page geometry, sides and shadow behaviour retain their authored measures.

Textures upload premultiplied, regenerate mipmaps, use **LINEAR_MIPMAP_LINEAR / LINEAR**, max mip **3**, clamped edges and anisotropy up to **4**. A serialized paint-set/clip/prefix signature prevents repaint/upload when nothing changed. Changed states repaint the whole small texture; partial uploads were optional and were not needed to express any build. Texture work is timed, including allocation on first use, paint, upload and mip generation. Actual cost is **unmeasured locally**.

**Continuations and every handover.** There is **no same-picture building-to-atlas handover**. Upper/lower maps retain their textures through `mapExport` **19.534094–20.034094** and `locExport` **20.394036–20.894036**, the dock thumbnails, the **21.194036350 AI→ID** transition and the ID carries. Map placement **22.667471350** deliberately becomes the assembled spread; location placement **25.232786705** deliberately becomes two composed page thumbnails. These use the specified historical before/after placement comparisons, not a claimed same-picture swap. The cover's main page, panel thumbnail, closed copies and turning cover retain `id:kCover` while that picture is visible, including export and sink. The wrapped phone post and tablet mail app remain their original atlas art. Figma's completed wires and their copies share the original use through **86.910256–87.310256**; `fill0/1/2` deliberately change those copies to other artwork at **87.531527 / 87.839075 / 88.111724**, while the original wires remain on their textures until the app leaves. Other picture uses remain on the atlas. **Fallbacks: none**; all eight builds are expressible with original paint commands.

**Removed pieces, pictures and pages.** No construction cutout/base/letter/prefix piece reaches the renderer for these building uses. Removed **210 generated cutout sprites**: **98 upper map, 44 lower map, 1 cover, 1 post, 2 mail, 24 desktop wire, 20 tablet wire, 20 phone wire**. The **four added bases** (`locGround`, `wireWideBase`, `wireTabletBase`, `wireTallBase`) remain internal recipe/ownership helpers but are no longer packed or rendered. Their added atlas pages **5 and 6** (**2048 × 1984 / 2048 × 412**) and all per-cutout runtime reveal textures are gone. The eight independent textures total **6,297,088 base bytes**. Original letter/prefix/base slots already on pages 0–4 remain reserved and painted to preserve those pages exactly; they have no construction rendering role.

Pages **0–4 are unchanged in packing and source paint**: pages 0–3 are **2048 × 1880 / 1700 / 1800 / 1992**; page 4 is **1492 in the Node model, 1480 in the browser**. All **303** places, sizes and mip phases remain. Page 1 remains **1700**, never 1704. The packed Node baseline is embedded in the checker, so tests do not depend on scratch notes. The original 254 drawings/8,447 paint calls also compare unchanged. This establishes unchanged page construction; actual raster-byte identity is not claimed as a local measurement. The external gate additionally compares SHA-256 of each page's real browser RGBA bytes with `dcb8094`.

**C7 result.** At **1 ms** through the actual ID sink **42.782352702–43.782352702**, **1,001 samples / 3,003 piece-edge checks** find all three spine-reaching pieces meeting at **x = −0.1**. Worst residual **3.0531133177191805e−16**, within the **1e−12** floating-point equality tolerance. No gap was found and no spine geometry changed. That attribution remains unrun, so the reported dark line is not locally classified.

### Round 17, critique round 4

**C8: measuring the group's ink.** `measureBuildingPaint()` records each finished source's actual commands without painting: transformed rectangle/path control hulls; centred stroke width, round/butt/square caps and conservative miter support; font ink metrics including overhang; existing source clips; and one atlas texel of antialiasing. Shadowed blobs additionally include their source shadow offsets and conservative three-blur-width support. Bounds are limited by the finished picture's own page clip, as the reference paint is. These source clips define the original picture and are not changed. No source drawing command, font, colour, picture size or atlas placement changes.

`buildingRevealSpec()` intersects each command's geometry with its authored region and extends only that command's owned paint to its stroke/AA bounds. A command spanning two Figma regions stays one command with the union of its clips; it is never revealed wholesale ahead of the other region. `buildingReveal()` retains the nominal start/edge, HAND progress, upward direction and eight bar steps, adding only the measured fringe. A fringe covering the final ink early does not retire a moving wire clip early: merging waits for its authored nominal completion. Completed playback recipes remain unclipped as before; the endpoint-review path explicitly retains their clips.

**Every padded reveal.** All values below are atlas texels at unchanged scale 1, in **[left, top, right, bottom]** order. ID ranges enumerate every affected definition. The audit has **228** source/group/region definitions: **162** need padding, **66** need none. Discrete marks that appear already complete keep their playback paint unchanged; their exact-progress-one review clips use the listed support. The source bounds are conservative command bounds, not a local raster measurement.

**Typing half-planes.** Every one of the **57** typed commands uses its original full command and typing count, with the moving right edge extended by **max(0, measured prefix right overhang) + 1 atlas texel**. The left/top/bottom sides remain half-planes containing all ink. Counts by source: **13 kMap / 34 kLoc / 2 kCover / 2 kPost / 6 eblast**. **Selection frames.** At **29.778904274**, the white-looking post-button selection is **four existing separate overlay bar pieces**, outside the building texture. Their existing geometry/tones are untouched; `selectionRelease` and `selectionLineWidth` identify them in `__hero.pieces`. The release mask projects each actual bar's centre line and its own line width, then strokes that line at **projected width + 1 px × DPR**. It never masks the rectangle's interior. At **85.928474558**, there is **no separate selection piece**: the wireframes' borders/crosses and heading-region paint belong to their building textures and are **not masked**. The mask helper rejects any purported selection piece carrying `art` or `building`.

**Endpoint review API.** `buildingLastState(id)` runs the same scheduler/painters at the last action's authored end, with progress exactly **1** and only that release withheld; its synchronous override is restored in `finally`. It retains the reveal clips, including full-count text half-planes. `__hero.buildings.lastState(id)` paints that recipe through `readPlan()` and returns the real mip readback and texture dimensions/signature. It does not seek to end-minus-epsilon or change normal playback. A later seek restores the normal paint signature.

At all **three sizes**—1474 × 996 DPR 1/2, 390 × 844 DPR 2—it now adds, for each of the **eight uses**, a last-state comparison at progress **1**, clips retained, levels **0–3**, maximum permitted channel difference **8**. The existing post-release readback remains **byte-identical (0)** at end + **.002 s**. A separate end − **.002 s** readback is a **record only**, including a deliberately missing final typed letter; it cannot fail a gate.

The **12** release instants remain at ± **.002 s**. Every release requires a nonempty own face in both frames at every size; ordinary releases also require a nonempty common mask. Photoshop's **2.296/2.991** rows explicitly include its photo despite its `echo: 'open'` metadata. Scoped release allowance stays **0 pixels over 8 levels**, inside the common own face grown **4 px × DPR**, excluding cursor, caret and only the separate selection strokes above. Whole-stage counts/percentages remain records. Other building faces and historical outside-face comparisons remain.

At placements **22.667471350/25.232786705**, both before and after frames compare with `dcb8094` **outside the carried and placed maps' projected faces**, within **8** levels. These masks use the actual faces without the ordinary release's 4-pixel growth, and include the composed map/page thumbnails. C7 now attributes **three** parallel pixel lines: the projected spine and offsets **−1/+1 CSS px × DPR perpendicular to it**, sampled densely enough not to skip device pixels. Every line must be nonempty; floor on **any** line fails. The **121** times **43.300–43.420**, at desktop DPR **1/2**, remain unchanged; side-wall/outline counts remain accepted records. The existing geometric spine check still finds **1,001 samples / 3,003 edges**, worst **3.0531133177191805e−16**, no geometry change.

**Fast and selected builds.** `--releases` runs only atlas hashes, post-release readback, the last-state/early readback, scoped releases and historical placements at all three sizes, plus readiness/offline error accounting. It skips builds, cost, stills, sinks and C7 attribution. Its requested approximate ten-minute duration is **unmeasured here**. Add `--uses` with comma-separated full use IDs and optional **loop-second** windows `id:from-to` to run only those builds, every **1/60 s**, taking pixel captures at **all three sizes**. Windows clamp to the use's original build interval. Without `--releases`, the remaining full-run gates stay; without `--uses`, the full run still takes build captures only at 1474 × 996 DPR 1. Invalid IDs, windows, duplicates and unknown options fail parsing. Examples, **external browser review only**:
