**Console performance warnings, from the code.** The explicit WebGL `gl.readPixels()` call is in `sq.mjs`'s `readBuildingLevels()`: four mip reads per call from `readback()`, `reference()` and the new `readPlan()/lastState()` review path. Normal playback never invokes these methods. These synchronous review readbacks can produce **“GPU stall due to ReadPixels”**. Separately, `art.mjs`'s `makeAtlas() → maskLetterPictures()` uses Canvas `getImageData()` for **54** reserved letter masks and **3** cached finished sources (**57** reads) during ordinary preview/player/stills initialization, despite those letter slots no longer rendering in the buildings. That normal startup path can produce the repeated **getImageData / willReadFrequently** warning; it is not a per-animation-frame read. `buildings.atlasHashes()` adds **5** page reads only when the review API calls it; the historical gate's `__r17Atlas()` also repacks/reads its reference atlas. Canvas readbacks can themselves entail a GPU stall internally, so the exact console message's origin cannot be distinguished without a browser trace. No readback-hint/cache/texture change was made merely to suppress warnings: preserving the atlas and source paint remains required.

**Verification and limits.** Required full Node suite: **all clean**, **6,582 frames / 470,872 pieces / max 187**, unchanged from critique 3; all **35** controls fail at their named gates. Final logs are `critique4-checks-final.txt` and `critique4-controls-final.txt`; changed modules pass `node --check`, including the pixel gate. The preservation audit (`critique4-audit.json`) deep-compares clocks, all **308** retained drawing definitions and **5,970** evenly spaced frames: **0 changed drawing commands / 0 changed geometry frames** (paint plans and the selection-identification metadata are the authorized differences). The loop remains **99.490894748 s**; all six scene starts, device models, picture geometry, colours, atlas packing and earlier C1/C2/C4/C5/C6/C7 fixes remain. K3 still checks **49** carried actions at **.002**, with all three negative controls caught.

No browser/pixel gate, screenshot, visual score, shader compilation, real-font raster-bound verification, GPU-byte result or cost/duration measurement is claimed. Those required browser gates are prepared for the separate review environment, as instructed. There is no design fallback or deliberate departure from C8; bounds are conservative where a command's exact raster support is unavailable in Node. The one-texel-plus-overhang font values and actual release pixel counts remain browser measurements, not synthetic evidence substituted for them.

For `[x,y,w,count]`, `motionCards()` returns a fresh array of `[x, y + i*w*.27*1.3, w, w*.27]` for each row. It mutates neither the table nor previous results. The offsets and `parked` shift are gone. Entrance x remains `restX + (1 - EASE.enter(linear(clipTime, ...LAYERS[i+3][5]))) * .75 * rowWidth`, with the original right-edge clip. Windows remain **1.2–1.7 / 1.35–1.85 / 1.5–2.0 s**. All words, ball path, keys, graph, sizes, row y/spacing, colours and original drawing commands outside the named cards remain. By 2.0 s all six comp pieces equal the built rest; 2.4 s and later retain that same end layout.

The AE viewer and thumbnail now retain animated clock **1** after Play instead of returning to construction; their head holds **2.4 s** until exported playback begins, which still starts at 0 and runs twice. The code audit also found a definite equivalent fault in Premiere: its monitor/head returned to **.9 s**, showing shot A/caption 2, while the exported last frame was **3.9 s**, graded shot C without a caption. C18-3 authorizes that rest-content fix: it now holds clock **1 / 3.9 s** until the existing export plays. No Premiere action time, footage, format layout or chrome changed.

**C18-1.4 fallback, explicit limit.** The head's pre-play drag is retained, from lane x **1.34** to **2.79** (1.45 clip seconds), over local **5.045305030–5.295305030 s**. Changing its endpoint to x **3.34** would increase the hand-rule duration **.25 → .28 s**, move `path` from **5.576539106 → 5.624716692 s**, shift all subsequent AE actions by **.048177585695 s** and the loop **99.490894747815 → 99.539072333510 s**. Those changes touch outside the timeline lane, so the brief says to keep the head. The complete built viewer remains the **2.0 s** frame while the pre-play head remains at **1.45 s**; this is an expressly reported exception, not a head/frame equality pass. During Play, after Play and during export, the head does match the displayed frame. The old after-play 1.6 s head is now 2.4 s.

Correcting `ROWS` would also change the `copies` cursor path if it still derived its anchors from the cards. Its old local-screen anchors **[2.5644,2.13544] → [2.6484,1.93888]** are therefore retained verbatim, keeping all routes and clocks bit-for-bit. These are cursor targets, not rest-card offsets. No invented timing override or new motion curve was needed.

**C18-3 consistency.** `tools/checks.mjs` runs the named `consistency` check, implemented/exported in `round17-checks.mjs` so the same gate can exercise in-memory mutants. It compares actual built/viewer/thumbnail pieces to `animatedComp(shape=3, clock=1)` in 480 × 270 source coordinates; checks 2.0/2.4/later endpoints; all five format rest sets; the original stagger/ease/distance during entrances; actual canvas card commands in all four stills and the two row tiles; and actual last-frame rectangles on all three exported devices. Rectangle tolerance is **.5 px**, worst observed **2.7711166694643907e-13 px**, **263 comparisons**. It checks **21** head/frame samples during/after play and export, including the Premiere hold and each exported last shot; **5** pre-play samples explicitly test and report the fallback. Fresh-return/mutation tests cover the pure rest helper. No pixel equality is claimed.

The new negative control adds **.09 rw** to card index 1's `animatedComp` rest x. It fails **`consistency: built/last rowB/4 axis 0`**, with **296 versus 308.6**. All **35** earlier controls remain and still fail at their named gates; **36** total. Mutants and private painter probes exist only in memory.

### Round 18, C18-4 / C18-5 follow-up

This follow-up supersedes run 1's **head fallback** and its **motion-mark / Premiere-poster uncertainties** above. C18-1–3's card stack, shared layout table, entrances, post-play holds and all other fixes remain. Before editing, read repository AGENTS.md, all of CORRECTIONS-18.md, then G/R2/R3 of CORRECTIONS-17.md, the existing Round 18 record and its acceptance/preservation notes. Acceptance for this run is recorded in `.scratch/review-out/r18/run2-acceptance.md`. The exact incoming source and schedule are saved in `run2-baseline.json` there. No external reference content was reused.

**C18-4: actual cause and correction.** `drawnFrame()` already has the correct board-space top-left anchor: board y goes **up**, so `[x, y+h*(1-p), w*p, h*p]` fixes the top edge. The later `buildingFaces()` pass consumes that piece and emits a full-size face with a canvas paint plan. Its `upCorner` clip used **downward** canvas v coordinates `[u,v+h*(1-p),u+w*p,v+h]`, fixing the bottom edge. That renderer-facing replacement caused the reported bottom-left growth. It now uses `corner`: `[u,v,u+w*p,v+h*p]`. Changing the corner helpers or moving the cursor would have introduced another error; neither changed.

The same audit found Figma's picture box and button using `wireRegion()`'s x-only reveal, which exposed their full height immediately. Those two calls now request `corner` too. The default remains `x` for typed fragments and the details action. Original paint, command order, mask padding, glyph weight, source sampling, face registration and final states remain, as G/R2/R3 and the later construction-texture corrections require. All other draw actions were already correctly anchored and were retained.

Every `kind: 'draw'` action is below, including `block` with its inherited rectangle tool, the tool-less AE marquee, and the two arrow-tool marquees. “Right now” means the Node corner/paint-plan check passes; it does not claim browser visual approval.

**New consistency gate and controls.** `checkDrawAnchors()` runs within the existing named `consistency` gate. It inventories **all 19 draws**, tests each at duration fractions **.20 / .35 / .50 / .65 / .80**, and compares both corners with the start and the actual emitted cursor tip. It examines emitted shape geometry and selection edges, and the **75 actual building-plan clips** used by the renderer, rather than trusting the superseded helper alone. Its independent expected rectangle comes from the action endpoints and HAND; existing C8 stroke/cap/AA padding is applied separately from the nominal rectangle. Tolerance is **0.5 native artwork px** for paint clips and AE geometry; selections without their own source use the 480-px-wide app-board scale. Worst error is **2.7711166694643907e-12 px** across **95** action samples. The existing **263** motion-layout comparisons and **21** play/post-play/export head samples remain.

The required new control gives **only frame0** the opposite `upCorner` base clip again; it fails at **`consistency: draw anchors fig/frame0`**. Two additional controls restore the picture box's x-only/full-height reveal and the 1.45 s head fallback; they fail at **`consistency: draw anchors fig/pictureBox`** and **`consistency: head reaches 2.0 s`** respectively. The earlier **.09 rw** card-offset control still fails at **`consistency:`**. All **36** incoming controls remain; **39** total fail at their intended gates. Probes and mutants exist only in memory, with no fixture edits or testing API added to the prototype.

**C18-5.1, the head.** The drag now ends at screen-local x **3.34** instead of **2.79**: **2.0** instead of **1.45** clip seconds from origin **1.34**. Its local start/end remain exactly **5.045305029997129 / 5.295305029997129 s**, a **.25 s** span. Its lane geometry uses **2.0 × handed(head)** and then parks at **2.0 s** before Play. Play still starts at 0, then holds **2.4 s** afterward.

The original scheduler route is retained when deriving the schedule, then only the head endpoint and the next cursor approach's origin are retargeted. That keeps the hand continuous without recalculating any beat. The ball action retains its exact `from`, `to`, three-point curve and **5.576539105853828 / 5.926539105853828 s** span; no later action endpoint or clock changes. The next approach naturally begins at the head's new endpoint, over its original span, and still reaches the same ball-path start. The hand-speed suite remains under **1500 px/s** (whole-loop peak **1499.7 px/s**, unchanged). The loop is still **99.49089474781469 s**, displayed as **99.491 s**. No fallback is needed.

**C18-5.2, editor marks.** `motWide`, `motTall` and `compWide` are never emitted as export faces. `screensOut()` uses the names only to select motion versus video, creates the device with no poster art, then calls `playback()` / `animatedComp()`. `chromeMotion` and `chromeStory` contain only chrome. `chromeMotionFeed` does draw `motSq` while assembling its atlas tile, then clears **[0,184,300,300]**. All **11** dotted-path discs and the **one** start key, including stroke and one texel of AA support, lie inside that later clear. No editor mark survives onto an exported screen at any clock. The manually drawn marks remain in the in-app viewer. Nothing needed removal from `art.mjs`.

**C18-5.3, Premiere posters.** `playerWide` and `reel` are never emitted as pictures; the export path selects `movie()` and `chromePlayer` / `chromeReel`. `feedSq` is invoked only as the chrome feed's underlay; its whole poster picture and burned `Move well.` caption are erased by the later **[0,112,300,300]** clear. Feed furniture outside the picture survives, while the live shot and separate `cap1` / `cap2` / `cap3` tiles supply the displayed caption. Thus no legacy poster caption is ever visible and none needs changing. This distinction between a drawing call and surviving output resolves the earlier “unsure” rows.

The added export-art check samples **1,184** frames, including all action boundaries and 30 Hz scene samples, and examines **3,540** exported picture pieces. It rejects direct use of all seven legacy still/poster names on export faces. Pure command/compositing checks prove that the **12** motion marks and **one** feed poster caption/plate are inside their later clears; player and reel chrome contain no caption text. This is command/geometry evidence, not a raster or font measurement.

**Preservation and verification.** `run2-preservation.mjs` independently loads the incoming and current stage modules. It proves all **115** action definitions/clocks/routes equal except **`ae/head.to`** and **`ae/path.previous`** as described above; all scene lengths, starts and held still times equal; and `art.mjs` byte-identical. Across **3,331** 30 Hz/action-end±.001 samples and **236,967** compared pieces, **3,150** full frames match exactly after rounding to 1e-10. The other **136 AE** samples differ only in the named head pieces/cursor interval, and **45 Figma** samples differ only in the five named draw actions' paint clips. ### Round 18, C18-6 / C18-7 follow-up

Built on the two retained C18-1–5 runs. Read repository AGENTS.md, all CORRECTIONS-18.md, then CORRECTIONS-17 G/R2/R3 before writing; carried forward README, the whole Round 18 record and run 2's acceptance/preservation evidence. Pre-edit acceptance is `.scratch/review-out/r18/run3-acceptance.md`; the incoming source and exact schedule are in `run3-baseline.json`. No original artwork, layout, colour, clock or device size changed.

**C18-6 cause: coplanar picture and lid, not a broken hinge or page crop.** In `lidPainter()`, the first picture's centre was **0.0195** along the lid normal relative to its centre plane. Its half-thickness is **0.0005**; the lid's front is **0.0200**. Consequently the picture's front was **0.0200**, exactly on the lid, and its back **0.0190**, inside the lid. Both pieces use separate superquadric meshes, so their coincident front surfaces compete for depth. This accounts for the fan-shaped lid/body fragments in the reported browser frames. `sq.mjs` uses `LEQUAL`, draws solids first and cut pictures afterward; moving `chromeLaptop` between `solid` and `cuts` would not clear the underlying opaque `uiPageWide0` picture from the lid. `poseLid()` already applies the same unit rotation and hinge to the lid and all its pictures, and `pageWindow()` already intersects geometry and UVs before rotation. Those mechanisms stay unchanged.

Node measurements from the incoming code, in lid-local units (full data in `run3-diagnosis-before.json`):

| Loop time | Lid angle | Face normal dot view | First picture back / front | Lid front | Finding |
| --- | ---: | ---: | --- | ---: | --- |
| 94.700 | -89.917938° | -0.537591 | hidden | .0200 | Back-facing pictures correctly omitted. |
| 94.900 | -3.704715° | .559815 | .0190 / .0200 | .0200 | Visible coplanar conflict. |
| 95.050 | 11.547348° | .691576 | .0190 / .0200 | .0200 | The pinned frame has the same zero front clearance. |
| 95.250 / 95.500 | 12° | .694779 | .0190 / .0200 | .0200 | Same latent overlap even once fully open. |
| 98.190895, shutting | -9.844514° | .495017 | .0190 / .0200 | .0200 | Shutting exposes the same fault. |
| 98.290895, shutting | -72.007249° | -.328056 | hidden | .0200 | Pictures correctly omitted after turning away. |

The current opening is **94.690894747815–95.090894747815 s**, shutting **98.090894747815–98.490894747815 s**. These are the unchanged current clocks; the approximate review-frame times are not a reason to retime the lid. Shutting has the same numerical fault while front-facing; whether it showed visible shards in the browser is unverified here.

**Correction.** Move the picture stack's origin **0.0195 → 0.0205**, including the chrome's reserved height. The first picture's back now rests on the glass at **0.0200** and its front is **0.0210**, clearing the lid by **0.0010**. Every screen layer moves exactly **0.001** along the lid normal; its in-plane rectangle, UV window, source picture, thickness **0.001**, layer step **0.0008** and chrome's eight-step reservation remain. No screen element is moved independently. `poseLid`, visibility, screen clipping, renderer passes, depth settings, hinge, -90°/12° angles, sizes and opening/shutting times are untouched.

**New gate and control.** `checkLidGlass()` runs within `checkRound17()`, which main `checks.mjs` already invokes. It samples each turn every **1/60 s**, both endpoints, and the reported **94.7 / 94.9 / 95.05 / 95.25 / 95.5 s** moments. Total **55** frames: **30** opening/rest samples (21 front-facing, 9 hidden), **25** shutting samples (9 front-facing, 16 hidden). It checks **103** emitted screen plates, **824** bounding vertices and **206** oriented bounding-face triangles. Each plate shares the lid basis, keeps its full local layout/crop/size at the same prototype clock, stays inside the glass rectangle, has a planar front and positive oriented area, and has its entire back at or above the lid face. The first back must sit on the glass and the front clear it by its **0.001** thickness. For the untapered sharp superquadrics, the full bounding plate contains all mesh vertices, so this clearance test prevents the actual cause without requiring a GPU raster test. The triangles counted here are the planar rectangle proof, not a claim to have read back GPU triangles.

Minimum back clearance is **-5.21e-17** (floating-point zero), front clearance **0.000999999999999949**; worst plane error **1.02e-16**, local-layout error **2.23e-16**, with **1e-9** tolerances. Restoring both origins to **0.0195** fails **`C18 lid glass:`** on the first visible opening sample's penetrating underside. The back-facing states are also tested and must emit no lid pictures. No renderer or pixel-gate edits were needed.

**Same-fault code audit.**

| Other place examined | Result |
| --- | --- |
| Figma phone/tablet prototype; Premiere/AE tablet and both phones; InDesign phone/tablet | Bodies and pictures have one fixed -90° orientation, with no independently moving lid. Existing C1 puts the first picture's back on the body's own glass and its front **0.002** above it. The retained glass gates pass **4,795** samples each; model/fill/aspect gates pass **4,817** device samples. No equivalent coplanar front remains. |
| InDesign's four turning booklet leaves | `leaf()` maps front/back artwork directly onto **14** paired strip volumes, rotating artwork and geometry together with their UV windows. There is no separate picture sitting on an opaque coplanar lid. Existing leaf/registration/spine checks remain clean; no change. |
| App badge tile flipping at every shell change | Front art belongs to the tile itself; the opposing back picture turns about the same pivot. Its outward face is **0.003** beyond the tile's back (**0.001** centre offset + **0.002** half-thickness), rather than coplanar with it. No change. |
| Other `turn()` uses, red morph blocks, bars and carried artwork | Bars have no picture. Red blocks have no picture and no independently moving face. Carried pictures translate as one piece. No other picture-bearing device part turns independently. |

**C18-7.** Removed the time-varying sine displacement from `place()` and removed `tremble`, `trembleHz` and `tremblePhase`. `S` now receives the authored `base` directly. Held stars have zero added displacement at every held duration; the mark still converges to its points and the ring retains its formation, fixed hashed slot scatter (`ringJitter`, not a time-varying shake), radius, joining order and rigid spin. Gathering and all release code are unchanged.

The charge does **not** feed release force: `blastImpulse()` uses held duration and mass, with its existing 2.5 s cap. `markState()` / `__hero.stars.mark` expose the charge, so its diagnostic ramp remains: **5.6 s** after formation, **2 s** ramp, maximum **1.5**; the old `hz` metadata is inert. The diagnostic no longer affects a visible position. This preserves the observable state without retaining the shake. Release still rebases from the star's shown position, now unshaken; there is no release jump.

Updated R4 checks test **1,800** frames through **30 s**, **2,142,360** held samples: every coordinate equals `Math.fround(base)` exactly, with and without the charge amplitude. **360,600** settled mark star-frames keep exactly the same uploaded coordinates; **456,159** settled ring star-frames stay within **0.000078653 px** of their previous position after undoing only the retained spin (tolerance **0.0002 px**, allowing float32 rounding). Additional **60 / 3,600 / 86,400 s** holds exercise both mark and no-mark fallback. All **1,359** captured stars release from their shown positions with **0 px** error. The new control restores the original hashed 10–16 Hz, 1.5 px sine displacement and fails **`C18 held stars:`**, star 35 at **8.017 s**. It runs in both the round 16 control set and required round 17 `--controls` command.

**Every earlier check changed in this follow-up, old → new.** Only `round16-checks.mjs` and `round17-checks.mjs` change functional checks; main `checks.mjs` changes only its now-obsolete tremble comment. No fixture, source artwork or pixel-gate file changed, and no prior tolerance was relaxed.

Chrome centre/front **.0259/.0264 → .0269/.0274**; every other lid-picture depth rises by the same **.001**. |

Charge start/full **8/10 s**, maximum **1.5**, mark formation **2.4 s**, and release tolerance **1e-6** remain unchanged. |

No other earlier check needed a new expectation: C18 consistency stays **263** rectangle comparisons, **21** play/head and **5** pre-play samples, **95** draw samples/**75** clips; export audit **1,184** frames/**3,540** pieces. ### Round 18, C18-8 follow-up

Built only C18-8 on the retained C18-1–7 code. Read repository AGENTS.md, the whole CORRECTIONS-18.md, then CORRECTIONS-17 G/R2/R3 before writing. Carried forward both READMEs, the existing Round 18 research/review record and run 3 acceptance/preservation evidence. Pre-edit acceptance is `.scratch/review-out/r18/run4-acceptance.md`; `run4-baseline.json` holds the incoming source and exact schedule. No source artwork or construction/release rule changed.

**Why the deck was blank.** Round 17 R10 allowed leaving keys/trackpad out while the lid was shut and turned away, to stop them showing through or holding up the flush lid. `poseLid()` implemented that option too broadly: `for (const pc of details) pc.hide = theta < 0`. `exportOut()` then removed all **47** detail pieces from the emitted frame until the lid passed upright, even while the lifting lid had already uncovered the deck. Shutting reversed that pop. C18-8 supersedes that omission option.

**Correction and enclosure.** Removed the angle switch, its unused parameter and the private `details` array link. The keyboard well, **45** keys and trackpad now remain drawn at their original places, sizes and colours in every frame. No new clip, renderer setting or geometry is needed: the existing opaque solid pass uses depth testing and depth writes with **LEQUAL**, at each piece's actual world depth, with **0** view-depth override. At a shared pixel the nearest surface wins; the lid itself hides the covered detail surfaces while the uncovered deck remains visible. The same mechanism works in reverse while shutting, rather than waiting for an angle threshold.

When shut, the deck top is **.080**, the lid spans **.080–.120**, the well/trackpad span **.081–.089**, and the keys span **.091–.096**. Every detail's entire bounding volume is inside the closed lid's central **96%** box: maximum normalized coordinate **.9500000000000196**, tolerance **1e-9**. This conservative box stays clear of the lid's rounded edges. A separate pure Node proof evaluates the renderer's actual uploaded sharp-mesh angle/index buffers with its vertex formula: all eight corners of that box lie inside all **960** nondegenerate outward triangle planes (**7,680** tests), minimum normalized clearance **.03668810093855402**. Because every detail is bounded by its eight corners, the closed opaque lid encloses the whole set. The mesh proof uses CPU evaluation, not GPU readback or pixels.

**New check and control.** `checkLidDeck()` runs inside `checkRound17()`, already called by main `checks.mjs`, under **`C18 lid deck:`**. It samples opening **94.690894747815–95.090894747815 s** and shutting **98.090894747815–98.490894747815 s** every **1/60 s**, with both endpoints and the pinned **94.91 s** frame: **26 + 25 = 51** frames. **15 / 20** samples exercise negative angles in the two directions. All **2,397** detail instances must be present, unhidden, at their unchanged positions/rotations/sizes/tones, without a turn-time clip. The shutting endpoint retains the original first-sink floor/body clips, which leave every detail wholly above the floor; those clips are not altered.

The gate invokes the actual renderer with a GL call recorder for each frame's **47** details and opaque lid: **7,344** submissions across colour, outline and shadow passes. Every submission must use the emitted position, rotation and size, depth testing, depth writes, **LEQUAL**, opaque mode, no blend/polygon offset and no view-depth lift. Colour/shadow expansion stays **0**; the original outline pass remains. This checks the depth contract for the full surfaces, rather than treating a finite set of sampled rays as a pixel proof. At both shut endpoints, **752** bounding vertices must be enclosed. Three synthetic Node cases independently exercise enclosure success, a missing detail and a detail protruding beyond the lid.

The new negative control reinstates the original `theta < 0` hiding and private detail link in memory. It fails **`C18 lid deck:`** on the shut opening endpoint's **0 versus 47** pieces. All **41** incoming controls still fail at their intended gates, including the C18-3/4 controls, C18-6 coplanar picture control and C18-7 tremble control: **42** total. No fixture or pixel-gate change.

**Every earlier check changed, old → new.** Only `tools/round17-checks.mjs` changes checks; main `checks.mjs` is byte-identical. No earlier tolerance is relaxed.

| Earlier check/record | Old → new |
| --- | --- |
| R10 `keys/trackpad above deck` | **0** emitted details above deck top **.080** while shut → **47** continuously drawn details enclosed by the shut lid **.080–.120**. Original well/trackpad top **.089 → .089**, key top **.096 → .096**. Replaced the old absence/height assertion with whole-volume enclosure; footprint/gap tolerances **.001 / 1e-6 → unchanged**, all hinge/angle/screen/sink assertions unchanged. |
| Shared renderer call recorder | Observed uniforms **4 → 8**, adding size, rotation, grow and near; also records depth function. Additional observations serve the new gate. C5's existing expected state, offset numbers and checks remain unchanged. |
| Required round 17 control inventory | **41 → 42**; added angle-hiding restoration, none removed. |
| Full-suite piece-count record | **470,687 → 476,797** (**+6,110 = 130 × 47** newly emitted detail instances). **6,582** frames, max **187**, five atlas pages and all acceptance thresholds unchanged. This is an output count, not a relaxed check. |

No other earlier check needed new numbers. Consistency retains **263** rectangle comparisons, **21 + 5** head samples and **95** draw samples/**75** clips; export audit **1,184** frames/**3,540** pieces. C18-6 retains **55** samples/**103** screen plates/**824** vertices/**206** triangles and the first picture's **.0200** back/**.0210** front. C18-7 and all earlier construction/artwork gates pass unchanged.

**Preservation and verification.** `run4-preservation.mjs` independently loads incoming/current modules over **3,382** frames. All **115** action definitions/routes/clocks, every scene length/start/still, and **238,829** unrelated emitted pieces are exactly equal, including every lid picture. **3,169** complete frames match exactly. The other **213** differ only in deck details: **100** frames add **4,700** previously omitted pieces, and **5,311** already-emitted detail instances retain every field except removal of inert `hide:false` metadata. The source comparison permits only the angle-switch/unused-data removal and comment. `art.mjs`, `sq.mjs`, `stars.mjs`, `kit.mjs`, `play.mjs`, `stills.mjs`, `view.mjs`, `labels.mjs` and main `tools/checks.mjs` are byte-identical.

`node --check` passes for stage, round 17 checks and both new Node probes. `run4-pure.mjs` passes the mesh/enclosure tests and new deck gate. Required `node design/journey-hero/take-2/tools/checks.mjs` exits **0**, ending **all clean**; required `node design/journey-hero/take-2/tools/round17-checks.mjs --controls` exits **0**, every one of **42** controls failing at its intended gate. Evidence: `.scratch/review-out/r18/run4-checks-final.txt`, `run4-controls-final.txt`, `run4-preservation.json` and `run4-pure.json`, with scripts/logs alongside.

The loop remains exactly **99.49089474781469 s**, PACE **1**. Scene starts remain **0 / 8.04631059223572 / 21.194036349675287 / 43.78235270175257 / 63.16364314101056 / 80.94573216333546 s**. All times, angles, sizes, places, pictures and colours are unchanged.

**Limits/departures.** No C18-8 code item remains open and no alternative design was substituted. Browser pixels, shader compilation and visual approval remain for the external review; the Node renderer recorder observes calls/state and does not rasterize.
